Code Module Memory

analysis

Root Cause : State machine protection was applied BEFORE all initialization was complete. Current: Materialized git codebook state for analysis at bccb16ce5356.

Directory docs/analysis
Source git:repoRoot=%2FUsers%2Fgecko%2Fsrc%2Fuvc&remoteUrl=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2Fjuergengeck%2Fuvc
Projected 2026-08-04T07:06:23.000Z
Updated 2026-08-04T07:06:23.000Z

Status And Memory

  1. Critical Issue Analysis & Fixes Applied learning

    Root Cause : State machine protection was applied BEFORE all initialization was complete. DeviceDiscoveryModel initialization happened AFTER protection, allowing failures to trigger backwards transitions. Fix Applied : Moved state machine protection to the very end of initialization in src/models/AppModel.ts: ```typescript // Initialize DeviceDiscoveryModel BEFORE applying state protection this. deviceDiscoveryModel = DeviceDiscoveryModel.getInstance(); await this. deviceDiscoveryModel.init(); // CRITICAL: Apply state machine protection LAST, after ALL initialization const originalTriggerEvent = this.state.triggerEvent.bind(this.state); this.state.triggerEvent = (event) = { if (event === 'shutdown' && this.isInitialized) { console.warn('[AppModel] Ignoring shutdown event preventing backwards transition'); return; } return originalTriggerEvent(event); }; ```

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  2. Critical Architecture Fix: Duplicate Connection Issue Resolved learning

    The lama app was failing during pairing with the error "No listening connection for the specified publicKey" because of a fundamental architecture flaw : we were creating two separate WebSocket connections to the CommServer with the same identity. 1. CommServerManager was creating its own WebSocket connection 2. ConnectionsModel was creating its own WebSocket connection 3. Both used the same publicKey and instanceId 4. CommServer got confused and only sent handover to one connection (ConnectionsModel) 5. When edda.one tried to pair back, CommServer couldn't route to the correct listening connection From the logs, we could see: Connection 1 (CommServerManager) : ✅ Authenticated successfully, ❌ Never received handover Connection 2 (ConnectionsModel) : ✅ Authenticated successfully, ✅ Received handover

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  3. Connection Timeout Root Cause Analysis & Fix learning

    After analyzing the logs, I found the exact cause of the EP KEYMISSMATCH and connection timeout errors: `` WARN [PairingService] Could not retrieve own public key: [TypeError: leuteModel.getOwnPublicKey is not a function (it is undefined)] `` 1. Critical cryptographic failure : The device couldn't retrieve its own public key 2. Connection handshake failure : Without public keys, secure connections cannot be established 3. EP KEYMISSMATCH errors : The server rejects connections when key validation fails 4. 30 second timeouts : Connection attempts hang because the cryptographic handshake never completes

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  4. 🎯 CRITICAL PAIRING FIX Root Cause Resolved learning

    Pairing between Lama mobile app and one.leute was failing in the encrypted handshake phase after successful WebSocket connection and initial protocol exchange. The issue was missing private keys in the cryptographic keychain: In the mobile environment, the default parameter key generation was failing to include private key components.

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  5. EP KEYMISSMATCH SUCCESS ANALYSIS learning

    1. No EP KEYMISSMATCH errors in the connection logs 2. Successful WebSocket connection establishment 3. Successful ONE protocol handshake 4. Connection shows as active : "isConnected": true 5. Exchange of Person IDs successful : Both local and remote Person IDs are now populated Connection Details: Local Person ID : d27f0ef1dd9e2588e283496bda4984d846ac777a86c6fa4337f357f28fa945df Remote Person ID : d83383d3243c519faaa9789d76cc1f319c7bbf9777e4fdcb8143bee8506cb4a4 Keys Hash : f76fbac7fa904ceb082c3732b52eaab859409fce1d255ee485de22541334fe98 Key Object Structure Discovery: Keys use publicKey and publicSignKey instead of encryptionKey and signKey Key object contains: ["$type$", "owner", "publicKey", "publicSignKey"]

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  6. ESP32 Ownership Provisioning Fix Summary learning

    1. Service Type Mismatch : The ESP32 firmware was sending provisioning ack on service type 11 (ESP32 RESPONSE SERVICE), but the app was expecting it on service type 2 (CREDENTIALS SERVICE). 2. Missing Owner ID : The ESP32's provisioning acknowledgment didn't include the owner ID, preventing the app from properly updating the device state. 3. Discovery Not Stopped : The ESP32 continued broadcasting discovery messages after being provisioned, making it appear unclaimed.

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  7. Invite Flow Root Cause Analysis learning

    The invite flow in our lama (Expo/React Native) implementation is broken because we completely replaced the working ConnectionsModel from one.models with a custom CommServerManager that doesn't implement the full pairing protocol. This means communication request messages from edda.one are received but never properly processed into contacts. // ✅ LeuteAccessRightsManager gets the ConnectionsModel this.LeuteAccessRightsManager = new LeuteAccessRightsManager( this.channelManager, this.connections, // ← ConnectionsModel passed here this.leuteModel ); ``` // ❌ LeuteAccessRightsManager never gets created because no ConnectionsModel // ❌ NetworkSettingsService tries to access appModel.connectionsModel doesn't exist ```

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  8. EP KEYMISSMATCH Issue Analysis & Solution learning

    After extensive debugging, the issue is confirmed to be at the ONE protocol key verification level , not WebSocket connectivity. Lama (mobile) logs: `` [PairingService] Default keys hash exists: true [PairingService] Keys are available for person d27f0ef1dd9e2588e283496bda4984d846ac777a86c6fa4337f357f28fa945df [PairingService] Connection attempt timed out after 30 seconds `` one.leute (web) error: `` Error: EP KEYMISSMATCH: Key does not match your previous visit at verifyAndExchangePersonId at async LeuteConnectionsModule.acceptConnectionViaCatchAll ``

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  9. 🔐 Keychain Corruption Analysis Pairing Failure Root Cause learning

    Pairing fails due to corrupted keychain entries that cannot be fixed with current one.core APIs. // ❌ DON'T: Rely on default parameters in mobile await createDefaultKeys(personId); ``` The pairing failure is caused by a fundamental keychain corruption issue in the mobile environment that cannot be fixed with current one.core APIs. The issue requires either:

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  10. Pairing Connection Fixes Summary learning

    This document summarizes the critical fixes applied to resolve pairing connection issues between the Lama mobile app and one.leute web application. // After: Protected with try catch try { const { webSocketMonitor } = require('../platform/webSocketMonitor'); const wsStats = { active: webSocketMonitor.getActiveConnections(), problematic: webSocketMonitor.getProblematicConnections(), total: webSocketMonitor.getAllStats() }; // ... logging } catch (wsError) { console.warn('[PairingService] Could not get WebSocket stats:', wsError); pairingDebugService.logPhase(debugAttemptId, 'websocket stats error', { error: 'Could not retrieve WebSocket stats' }, false, wsError as Error); } ``` // After: Only check reliable properties const successfulConnection = connections.find(conn = conn.isConnected === true && conn.remotePersonId && conn.remotePersonId.length 0 ); ```

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  11. Pairing Root Cause Analysis & Resolution learning

    CRITICAL ISSUE IDENTIFIED : The pairing process was failing due to missing private keys in the cryptographic keychain, causing the encrypted handshake phase to stall indefinitely. WebSocket Monitor Logs Showed : `` 📤 BINARY: ws xxx (72 bytes) Initial encrypted exchange 📥 BINARY: ws xxx (72 bytes) 📤 BINARY: ws xxx (207 bytes) Larger protocol messages 📥 BINARY: ws xxx (139 bytes) ... continues for 60 seconds but never completes `` Key Analysis Revealed the Problem : `` 🔑 Key Object Analysis: Has encryptionKey: false ❌ Has signKey: false ❌ Has communionKey: false ❌ Key object type: object Key object keys: ["$type$", "owner", "publicKey", "publicSignKey"] ``

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  12. QUICVC Migration Summary learning

    Before: Used service type 7 packets for VC exchange `` [0x07] [JSON payload] // Service type byte + raw JSON `` After: Uses QUICVC INITIAL packets with VC INIT frames `` [QUICVC header] [VC INIT frame (0x10)] [frame length] [JSON payload] `` Before: Only handled provisioning and revocation Did not understand vc request messages Could not respond with device status

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  13. 🔐 Secret Key Storage Analysis The Real Root Cause learning

    The pairing failure is NOT due to key generation issues, but due to SECRET KEY STORAGE FAILURE in the mobile environment. 1. Public Keys (Keys object): Stored in main database as unversioned object Contains: $type$, owner, publicKey, publicSignKey Always accessible once created 2. Secret Keys (Private files): Stored separately in private storage as encrypted files Files: <keysHash .encrypt, <keysHash .sign Encrypted with master key before storage Must exist for keys to be considered "default"

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

  14. UDP Packet Truncation Bug Analysis & Fix learning

    1. VC Truncation : ESP32 receives 0x07 0x7b 0x22 0x74 (4 bytes) instead of full 118 byte VerifiableCredential request 2. Four Zeros : ESP32 receives 0x00 0x00 0x00 0x00 (4 zero bytes) 1. Type conversion error in JSI layer (JavaScript number → C++ size t) 2. Buffer view corruption where finalLength gets set to 4 instead of 118 3. Memory alignment issue during JSI parameter marshalling // ADDITIONAL SAFETY CHECK: Ensure buffer and length parameters are consistent if (finalBuffer.byteLength !== finalLength) { finalLength = finalBuffer.byteLength; } ```

    How to apply: Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

    explanationactiveRoot Cause : State machine protection was applied BEFORE all initialization was complete. DeviceDiscoveryModel initialization happened AFTER protection, allowing failures to trigger backwards transitions. Fix Applied : Moved state machine protection to the very end of initialization in src/models/AppModel.ts: ```typescript // Initialize DeviceDiscoveryModel BEFORE applying state protection this. deviceDiscoveryModel = DeviceDiscoveryModel.getInstance(); await this. deviceDiscoveryModel.init(); // CRITICAL: Apply state machine protection LAST, after ALL initialization const originalTriggerEvent = this.state.triggerEvent.bind(this.state); this.state.triggerEvent = (event) = { if (event === 'shutdown' && this.isInitialized) { console.warn('[AppModel] Ignoring shutdown event preventing backwards transition'); return; } return originalTriggerEvent(event); }; ```Critical Issue Analysis & Fixes AppliedCritical Issue Analysis & Fixes Applied

    explanationactiveThe lama app was failing during pairing with the error "No listening connection for the specified publicKey" because of a fundamental architecture flaw : we were creating two separate WebSocket connections to the CommServer with the same identity. 1. CommServerManager was creating its own WebSocket connection 2. ConnectionsModel was creating its own WebSocket connection 3. Both used the same publicKey and instanceId 4. CommServer got confused and only sent handover to one connection (ConnectionsModel) 5. When edda.one tried to pair back, CommServer couldn't route to the correct listening connection From the logs, we could see: Connection 1 (CommServerManager) : ✅ Authenticated successfully, ❌ Never received handover Connection 2 (ConnectionsModel) : ✅ Authenticated successfully, ✅ Received handoverCritical Architecture Fix: Duplicate Connection Issue ResolvedCritical Architecture Fix: Duplicate Connection Issue Resolved

    explanationactiveAfter analyzing the logs, I found the exact cause of the EP KEYMISSMATCH and connection timeout errors: `` WARN [PairingService] Could not retrieve own public key: [TypeError: leuteModel.getOwnPublicKey is not a function (it is undefined)] `` 1. Critical cryptographic failure : The device couldn't retrieve its own public key 2. Connection handshake failure : Without public keys, secure connections cannot be established 3. EP KEYMISSMATCH errors : The server rejects connections when key validation fails 4. 30 second timeouts : Connection attempts hang because the cryptographic handshake never completesConnection Timeout Root Cause Analysis & FixConnection Timeout Root Cause Analysis & Fix

    explanationactive1. Service Type Mismatch : The ESP32 firmware was sending provisioning ack on service type 11 (ESP32 RESPONSE SERVICE), but the app was expecting it on service type 2 (CREDENTIALS SERVICE). 2. Missing Owner ID : The ESP32's provisioning acknowledgment didn't include the owner ID, preventing the app from properly updating the device state. 3. Discovery Not Stopped : The ESP32 continued broadcasting discovery messages after being provisioned, making it appear unclaimed.ESP32 Ownership Provisioning Fix SummaryESP32 Ownership Provisioning Fix Summary

DeviceDiscoveryModel initialization happened AFTER protection, allowing failures to trigger backwards transitions.     learningCritical Architecture Fix: Duplicate Connection Issue ResolvedThe lama app was failing during pairing with the error "No listening connection for the specified publicKey" because of a fundamental architecture flaw : we were creating two separate WebSocket connections to the CommServer with the same identity. 1. CommServerManager was creating its own WebSocket connection 2. ConnectionsModel was creating its own WebSocket connection 3. Both used the same publicKey and instanceId 4. CommServer got confused and only sent handover to one connection (ConnectionsModel) 5. When edda.one tried to pair back, CommServer couldn't route to the correct listening connection From the logs, we could see: Connection 1 (CommServerManager) : ✅ Authenticated successfully, ❌ Never received handover Connection 2 (ConnectionsModel) : ✅ Authenticated successfully, ✅ Received handoverDerived from an explanatory memory so current module orientation stays available.Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

    explanationactiveThe lama app was failing during pairing with the error "No listening connection for the specified publicKey" because of a fundamental architecture flaw : we were creating two separate WebSocket connections to the CommServer with the same identity. 1. CommServerManager was creating its own WebSocket connection 2. ConnectionsModel was creating its own WebSocket connection 3. Both used the same publicKey and instanceId 4. CommServer got confused and only sent handover to one connection (ConnectionsModel) 5. When edda.one tried to pair back, CommServer couldn't route to the correct listening connection From the logs, we could see: Connection 1 (CommServerManager) : ✅ Authenticated successfully, ❌ Never received handover Connection 2 (ConnectionsModel) : ✅ Authenticated successfully, ✅ Received handoverCritical Architecture Fix: Duplicate Connection Issue ResolvedCritical Architecture Fix: Duplicate Connection Issue Resolved

    learningConnection Timeout Root Cause Analysis & FixAfter analyzing the logs, I found the exact cause of the EP KEYMISSMATCH and connection timeout errors: `` WARN [PairingService] Could not retrieve own public key: [TypeError: leuteModel.getOwnPublicKey is not a function (it is undefined)] `` 1. Critical cryptographic failure : The device couldn't retrieve its own public key 2. Connection handshake failure : Without public keys, secure connections cannot be established 3. EP KEYMISSMATCH errors : The server rejects connections when key validation fails 4. 30 second timeouts : Connection attempts hang because the cryptographic handshake never completesDerived from an explanatory memory so current module orientation stays available.Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

    explanationactiveAfter analyzing the logs, I found the exact cause of the EP KEYMISSMATCH and connection timeout errors: `` WARN [PairingService] Could not retrieve own public key: [TypeError: leuteModel.getOwnPublicKey is not a function (it is undefined)] `` 1. Critical cryptographic failure : The device couldn't retrieve its own public key 2. Connection handshake failure : Without public keys, secure connections cannot be established 3. EP KEYMISSMATCH errors : The server rejects connections when key validation fails 4. 30 second timeouts : Connection attempts hang because the cryptographic handshake never completesConnection Timeout Root Cause Analysis & FixConnection Timeout Root Cause Analysis & Fix

    learningESP32 Ownership Provisioning Fix Summary1. Service Type Mismatch : The ESP32 firmware was sending provisioning ack on service type 11 (ESP32 RESPONSE SERVICE), but the app was expecting it on service type 2 (CREDENTIALS SERVICE). 2. Missing Owner ID : The ESP32's provisioning acknowledgment didn't include the owner ID, preventing the app from properly updating the device state. 3. Discovery Not Stopped : The ESP32 continued broadcasting discovery messages after being provisioned, making it appear unclaimed.Derived from an explanatory memory so current module orientation stays available.Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

Service Type Mismatch : The ESP32 firmware was sending provisioning ack on service type 11 (ESP32 RESPONSE SERVICE), but the app was expecting it on service type 2 (CREDENTIALS SERVICE). 2.     learningInvite Flow Root Cause AnalysisThe invite flow in our lama (Expo/React Native) implementation is broken because we completely replaced the working ConnectionsModel from one.models with a custom CommServerManager that doesn't implement the full pairing protocol. This means communication request messages from edda.one are received but never properly processed into contacts. // ✅ LeuteAccessRightsManager gets the ConnectionsModel this.LeuteAccessRightsManager = new LeuteAccessRightsManager( this.channelManager, this.connections, // ← ConnectionsModel passed here this.leuteModel ); ``` // ❌ LeuteAccessRightsManager never gets created because no ConnectionsModel // ❌ NetworkSettingsService tries to access appModel.connectionsModel doesn't exist ```Derived from an explanatory memory so current module orientation stays available.Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.

    learning🔐 Secret Key Storage Analysis The Real Root CauseThe pairing failure is NOT due to key generation issues, but due to SECRET KEY STORAGE FAILURE in the mobile environment. 1. Public Keys (Keys object): Stored in main database as unversioned object Contains: $type$, owner, publicKey, publicSignKey Always accessible once created 2. Secret Keys (Private files): Stored separately in private storage as encrypted files Files: <keysHash .encrypt, <keysHash .sign Encrypted with master key before storage Must exist for keys to be considered "default"Derived from an explanatory memory so current module orientation stays available.Use this explanation to orient changes before widening scope.
